What about "topological" sequences from De Bruijn? Pair sums flat if set is a difference set? Difference sets are nonnegative and can have cyclic sum flat if P^2 cyclic constant. Do there exist polynomials with nonnegative coefficients P(x) such that P(x)^2 mod (x^N-1) has constant coefficients? This is a "clumping" or "perfect autocorrelation"? Let's search memory: Polynomial P of length N with nonnegative integer coefficients whose square modulo x^N-1 has constant coefficients λ. Let's try design a heuristic that explicitly targets low convolution via "avoidance" of high-sum pairs. The multiplicative gradient with softmax on c will reduce weights of elements involved in high-sum coefficients. This is like repulsive force: each element i and j with sum k high repel. It may produce a structure where no pair products high; likely a B_2-like set with lower weights on colliding pairs. This could find good sequences. We can run many iterations from random and then coordinate. This may be our differentiator.

Let's specify multiplicative repulsion algorithm more concretely:
- Work with a_i positive (floor eps). Normalize sum=1.
- Define soft peak P_β(a)= (1/β) logsumexp(β c_k / S²?) Since S=1, use c. But c values ~1/n. For β maybe 10*n to emphasize peak. Alternatively use normalized c_k * n (probability density). Let d_k = c_k * n, average ~0.5; peak ~target ~1.5/2=0.75? For score 1.5, max d≈0.75. Use β around 20-100.
- Gradient of soft F: w_k softmax(β d_k). g_i = 2∑_k w_k a_{k-i}. Then do a_i ← a_i * exp(-η g_i / \bar{g}) to keep sum approx. This is analogous to gradient descent on log a for smooth max. Since F scale invariant? If we normalize after, okay.
- This update tends to reduce involved pairs. If a_i small, exp factor. We can add small noise to logs to avoid zeros: loga += normal(0,σ). Anneal σ down/up.
- After each block, run a few coordinate sweeps if quality improves.

Will this escape uniform? Start random not uniform. If uniform, gradient uniform; add noise or start random. Good.

We can implement a generic "repulsion" loop:
```
def repulsion_search(a, budget, rng):
  a=clip positive sum1
  best=copy; score=eval_fast
  # loga = log(a) with eps
  for iter in time budget:
     beta = base_beta * (1 + 0.1*iter?) maybe increase gradually
     c = conv_fast(a)
     # normalize d = c / c.mean? sum c=1; mean=1/(2n-1). d = c * (2n-1) maybe.
     w = softmax(log(d)*beta) # careful overflow; d small, beta* d maybe <100
     w = convolve weights? Use FFT correlation.
     # gradient g length n
     # Update logs: loga -= eta*g / np.mean(g)
     # eta schedule: e.g., 0.05/(1+iter/100)
     # Add noise: loga += rng.normal(0, noise, n)
     # Normalize logs (subtract logsumexp) to sum1
     a = exp(loga)
     # coordinate polish every few iterations?
```
Need compute g via FFT accurately. Alternatively, since n may be up to 512, direct g computation using weights and a via convolution matrix can be vectorized: g_i = ∑_{j=0}^{n-1} a_j w_{i+j}. Implement FFT correlation:

```
def eval_t(i, t, c, c_seg, a_seg, max_unaff, S, old, n, self_pos):
   dt=t-old
   if dt==0: return c_max? max_unaff? maybe
   delta = 2*dt*a_seg
   delta[self_pos] += dt*dt
   newmax = new_seg.max()
   if max_unaff > newmax: newmax=max_unaff
   sumnew = S - old + t
   return 2*n*newmax/(sumnew*sumnew)
```

Internal fast score:
```
def fast_score(a):
    n=len(a)
    if n<1 or sum<=0: return inf
    L=1; while L<2*n-1: L*=2
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,L)[:2*n-1]
    c=np.maximum(c,0)
    m=float(np.max(c))
    s=float(np.sum(a))
    if s<1e-12: return inf
    return float(2*n*m/(s*s))
```

Central sequence generation:
```
def central_sequence(n):
    # a_m = C(2m,m)/4^m recursively: a_0=1, a_m = a_{m-1} * (2m-1)/(2m)
    a=np.empty(n); a0=1; a[0]=1
    for m in range(1,n): a[m]=a[m-1]*(2*m-1)/(2*m)
    # optional clip positive
    return a
```

Let's implement shape_search function:
```
def shape_search(base, n, budget, seed, beta_start=0.5, beta_end=20, step_start=0.05):
    x = np.linspace(0,1,n)
    # choose basis: maybe Legendre polynomials up to deg 10 on [0,1], plus sqrt boundary features.
    R = min(20, max(6, n//256?))
    V = build_basis(n)
    Vp = np.linalg.pinv(V)
    u = zeros(R)
    logbase=log(base+eps)
    # to avoid large exp, center loga
    loga = logbase.copy()
    best_a = base.copy(); best_score=fast_score(base)
    for iter:
       a=clip(exp(loga - logsumexp(loga?))? Scaling irrelevant; but base large a0=1, sum large; loga values okay. To avoid overflow, we can normalize a to sum1 for computation? Score scale invariant; loga can be shifted each iter without changing u? If we shift all loga by constant, equivalent to adding constant parameter; nuisance. We can instead normalize a for score only: a_norm=a/S; update loga with subtract mean? Simpler: keep loga = logbase+V u - mean(logbase+Vu) (center) so sum approx central, not zero. But scale irrelevant; centering prevents overflow and uses constant direction. For score, use a directly (scale doesn't matter). Ensure max loga not >50.
       d = V@u (center subtract mean)
       a=base*exp(d - mean(d))
       # compute c, dnorm = c / c.max(); weights softmax(beta*dnorm)
       c=fft_conv(a) (or for n large use FFT, length power)
       w=softmax(beta*(c/cmax - log(csum? maybe d / mean?)))
       # use d = c / (c.sum()/(2n-1))? softmax(beta*d) where d mean1.
       corr=correlation_fft(w,a) # length n: corr_i=∑ w_{i+j} a_j
       h=a*corr  # omit 2
       delta = -h / (np.linalg.norm(h) or mean(abs))
       # project to basis
       du = Vp.T? If Vp shape R×n, du=Vp @ delta
       # Since V is n×R, pinv shape R×n, du=Vp.dot(delta)
       # scale du to desired log change norm ~ step
       norm=norm(du); if >0: du = step * du/norm
       # add random small to u
       u += du + noise*eta
       # cleanup
       a_new=base*exp(V@u - mean)
       score=fast_score(a_new)
       update best
```

Coordinate search function:
```
def coordinate_polish(a, budget, rng, max_sweeps=None):
    n=len(a)
    a=np.asarray(a,float); a=np.clip(a,0,None)
    if sum <=0: return a, inf
    c=np.convolve(a,a) # for n up to maybe 2048/4096; if n>4096 use FFT? Direct better exact.
    S=float(a.sum()); cur=score_from_c(n,c,S)
    best=a.copy(); best_sc=cur
    deadline
    sweep=0
    while time<deadline:
      order = np.roll(np.arange(n), rng.integers(n)) + maybe random step? Use sequential last coord? 
      for i in order:
         # line search
         old=a[i]
         # if current score already very close? still
         t_best = line_search_coord(...)
         if t_best != old:
            update c, a, S
            if score<cur: cur=...
            # Occasionally rescale? No.
      best track
      if no improvement in sweep and sweep > ... maybe perturb random block
```

```
def coord_candidates(i, old, S, n, c, c_seg, a_seg, max_unaff):
   ts=[0.0, old, S/n]
   if old>0:
      for r in (0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.25,1.5,2.0,4.0,8.0): ts.append(old*r)
   else:
      for f in (0.1,1.0,10.0,100.0): ts.append(S/n*f)
   # Bound hi
   hi=max(10*S/n, 10*old if old>0 else 0, 1.0) # but a0 maybe 1; use max coord?
   # Evaluate candidates. If best at high maybe expand.
```

```
def coord_line_search(i, a, c, S, n):
    old = float(a[i]); c_seg=c[i:i+n]; a_seg=a[i:i+n]; pos=i
    compute max_unaff
    cur_score = 2*n*float(np.max(c))/(S*S)
    # candidate list in Python list
    cand = [old,0.0]
    mean = S/n
    if old>0: for r in ratios: cand.append(old*r)
    else: for f in factors: cand.append(mean*f)
    # bound hi
    hi = max(mean*4, old*4 if old>0 else 0, 1.0)  # if old large? use max coord?
    # Ensure cand includes hi
    best_t=old; best_sc=cur_score
    for t in cand:
       if t<0 or not finite: continue
       # score compute
    # If best at 0 or maybe at hi, bracket
    lo=0.0; hi=max(best_t*4.0, hi, 1e-12)
    # Ensure endpoints not both? Do 12 golden
    gr=(sqrt5-1)/2
    c1=hi-gr*hi; c2=gr*hi; f1=score(c1); f2=score(c2)
    for _ in range(12): ... if f1<f2: hi=c2;c2=c1;f2=f1; c1=hi-gr*hi; f1=score(c1) else: lo=c1;...
    best among golden
    return best_t if score<cur_score else old
```

Score computation in line search repeated; to avoid code duplication, we can have a local inline via a small top-level function `coord_t_score(c_seg,a_seg,max_unaff,S,n,old,t,pos)`. Function call overhead maybe acceptable for n≤2048 (tens thousands). Let's implement top-level:
```
def coord_t_score(c_seg, a_seg, max_unaff, S, n, old, t, pos):
    dt=t-old
    if dt==0:
       newmax = max(max_unaff, float(c_seg.max()))
    else:
       # need new_seg = c_seg + 2dt*a_seg; adjust pos
       # using tmp = c_seg + 2dt*a_seg allocates
       tmp = c_seg + 2.0*dt*a_seg
       if dt != 0.0:
          tmp[pos] += dt*dt
       newmax = float(tmp.max())
       if max_unaff > newmax: newmax=max_unaff
    sumnew = S - old + t
    if sumnew <=0: return inf
    return (2.0*n*newmax)/(sumnew*sumnew)
```

Random initializations:
```
def random_smooth_init(n, seed, bias=0.0):
   rng=default_rng
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   # log smooth: random low freq
   f = np.zeros(n)
   for mode in 1..5: f += rng.normal(0, 1/mode)*cos(2π mode x + phase)
   a = np.exp(2*f) * (1+bias*x)^2
   a=np.clip(a,1e-6,None)
   return a
```

Repulsion full search (log multiplicative) maybe:
We can implement a simpler stochastic multiplicative update that doesn't use basis, for n≤2048:
```
def repulsion_loop(a, budget, rng):
   a normalized max~1; c=conv
   beta=2 -> 20 over iter
   eta=0.1 decays
   loga=log(a+eps)
   for it:
      c=fft_conv(a)
      w=softmax(beta*c/cmax)
      corr=correlation(w,a)
      h=a*corr
      # log update: loga -= eta*h/mean(h)
      # add noise
      loga += rng.normal(0, noise, n)
      a=clip(exp(loga)); normalize
      # occasionally coordinate single
      eval update
```
This is similar to shape_search but full coordinates. It may escape basins. Use for medium n. Time: each iter two FFTs n=1024/2048, cheap; many iters. Could find good global-ish.

Need implement correlation FFT:
```
def correlation_fft(w, a):
   n=len(a); L=1 while L<2*n: L*=2
   A=np.fft.rfft(a,L); W=np.fft.rfft(w,L)
   r=np.fft.irfft(A*np.conj(W), L).real
   return r[:n]
```
Careful: w length 2n-1. L>=2n-1 enough. `conj` of rfft okay. Test orientation: r_i = ∑_j a_j w_{(i+j) mod L}. For i+j up to 2n-2 < L, good.

Softmax:
```
def softmax_from_values(vals, beta):
   z = beta*vals
   z -= z.max()
   e=np.exp(z); return e/e.sum()
```
If vals normalized by cmax, beta <=50; z range [0,beta]. okay.

Fast convolution:
```
def fft_convolution(a):
   n=len(a); L=1; while L<2*n-1: L*=2
   A=np.fft.rfft(a,L); return np.fft.irfft(A*A,L)[:2*n-1]
```
For coordinate direct, use initial direct c from `np.convolve(a,a)`. For repulsion, use FFT.

```
    cur=score_from_c(n,c,S)
    best=a.copy(); best_sc=cur
    sweeps=0
    last_improve=...
    while time<deadline:
       order = np.arange(n)
       rng.shuffle(order) maybe start random
       imp=0
       for idx in order:

           t = coord_line_search(idx,a,c,S,n)
```

```
def random_smooth_base(n,rng):
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   f=np.zeros(n)
   for k in range(1,6): f += rng.normal(0,1.0/k)*np.cos(2*np.pi*k*x + rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
   y=rng.normal(0,0.5)
   a=np.exp(f+y*x)
   # combine with central to ensure decay? maybe a=0.2*central +0.8*a? nonneg
   return a
```

```
def try_structural_move(a, c, S, n, rng, cur_score, deadline, max_trials):
```

Repulsion function code sketch:
```
def repulsion_search(a0, rng, deadline, beta_start=2.0, beta_end=30.0, eta=0.05, noise=0.02, verbose=False):
    a=normalize_sum1(a0); n=len(a)
    L=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    best=a.copy(); best_sc=fast_score_from_c? compute c via fft
    loga=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-12))
    # reference log range
    logmin=loga.min()-5; logmax=loga.max()+5
    iters=0
    while time<deadline:
       iters+=1
       beta=beta_start + (beta_end-beta_start)*min(1,iters/max(1,int(deadline/time_per?))) # simpler based on iters total unknown; use min(1, iters/1000)
       a=np.exp(loga)
       a=a/a.sum()
       A=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
       c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,L)[:2*n-1]
       c=np.maximum(c,0)
       cmax=float(c.max())
       if cmax<=0: break
       d=c/cmax
       z=beta*d
       z-=z.max()
       w=np.exp(z); w/=w.sum()
       W=np.fft.rfft(w,L)
       corr=np.fft.irfft(A*np.conj(W),L)[:n]
       h=a*corr
       scale=float(np.mean(h)) if np.mean(h)>0 else 1.0
       eta_cur=eta*(0.5+0.5/min(1.0,iters/50?))
       loga -= eta_cur * h / scale
       loga += rng.normal(0.0, noise*(eta_cur*20? ), n)
       # clip loga
       loga=np.clip(loga, logmin, logmax)
       sc=2*n*cmax  # since sum=1? c from a sum1, yes score=2n*cmax
       if sc<best_sc: best=a.copy(); best_sc=sc
       # cheap coordinate? no
    return best, best_sc
```

```
def coord_scores_for_ts(c_seg, max_unaff, S, n, old, pos, ts):
```

Signature: `coord_scores_for_ts(c_seg, a_seg, max_unaff, S, n, old, pos, ts)`.
Implementation:
```
K=len(ts)
if K==0: return empty
dt=ts-old
mat = c_seg.reshape(1,-1) + (2.0*dt).reshape(-1,1)*a_seg.reshape(1,-1)
self_add = dt*dt
mat[:, pos] += self_add
rowmax = np.max(mat, axis=1)
unaff = max_unaff
if unaff > rowmax: rowmax = np.maximum(rowmax, unaff) # broadcasting
sumnew = S - old + ts
# if sumnew<=0, score inf
scores = np.full(K, np.inf)
valid=sumnew>0
scores[valid] = 2.0*n*rowmax[valid]/(sumnew[valid]*sumnew[valid])
return scores
```
Self_add broadcasting: `mat[:, pos] += dt*dt` works. For `pos` out of range? pos=i < n yes. If K large, mat size K*n. Good. For K=2 golden, overhead small but many calls; okay. Could write scalar eval for golden to avoid matrix, but matrix K=2 okay.

Let's code local_search top-level with parameters:
```
def local_search_sequence(a0, rng, deadline, verbose=False, max_sweeps=200):
    a=normalize_sum1(a0)
    n=len(a)
    if n==0: return a
    c = np.convolve(a,a) if n<=3000 else fft_conv(a); c=np.clip(c,0,None)
    S=1.0
    cur_score = 2.0*n*float(np.max(c))  # since S=1
    best=a.copy(); best_score=cur_score
    sweeps=0; no_improve=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline and sweeps<max_sweeps:
       # coordinate sweep
       order = np.arange(n)
       if sweeps % 2 ==0:
          # use random roll, not full shuffle for speed
          start=int(rng.integers(0,n)); order=np.roll(order,start)
       else:
          order=rng.permutation(n)
       improved=0
       for idx in order:
           if time.monotonic()>=deadline: break
           res = coord_line_search(int(idx), a, c, S, n, cur_score)
           if res is None: continue
           t,newsc = res
           if newsc < cur_score - 1e-13:
              i=int(idx); old=a[i]; dt=t-old
              if dt != 0.0:
                  if 0 <= 2*i < c.size:
                      c[i:i+n] += 2.0*dt*a[i:i+n]
                      c[2*i] += dt*dt
                  a[i] = t
                  S += dt
              else: a[i]=t; S+=dt
              cur_score=newsc
              improved +=1
              if newsc < best_score - 1e-13:
                  best_score=newsc
                  best=a.copy()
       sweeps +=1
       # rescale to sum1
       if S>1e-12:
          factor=1.0/S
          a*=factor
          c*=factor*factor
          S=1.0
       best = best / max(1.0, np.sum(best))? To store normalized, do best=best/best.sum()
       if improved ==0:
          no_improve +=1
       else:
          no_improve=0
       if no_improve >= 3:
          # structural moves
          a,c,S,cur_score,accepted = structural_move(a,c,S,n,rng,cur_score,deadline)
          if accepted:
             no_improve=0
             # recompute best if cur_score < best?
          else:
             # if no improvement, break after some attempts
             if no_improve >= 8: break
       # occasional recompute c direct to correct
       if sweeps % 20 ==0 and n<=3000:
          c=np.convolve(a,a); c=np.clip(c,0,None); S=1.0
          cur_score=2*n*max(c)
    # normalize best sum1? score invariant. Return best/sum(best)
    bs=np.sum(best)
    if bs>0: best=best/bs
    return best,best_score
```
But `structural_move` may return same a with different orientation; need update best if score same? We can set if cur_score < best_score or (not improved but no_improve) maybe best unchanged. If structural improves, best update.

`structural_move` top-level:
```
def structural_move(a,c,S,n,rng,cur_score,deadline):
   best_a=a.copy(); best_c=c.copy(); best_S=S; best_sc=cur_score; accepted=False
   for trial in range(12):
      if time>deadline: break
      choice=int(rng.integers(0,4))
      if choice==0:
         # reverse only if n? same score; accept if no_improve high; return true to change basin
         new_a=a[::-1].copy(); new_c=c[::-1].copy()
         # keep sum S
         # score same, but consider best_sc (maybe due roundoff)
         # To avoid endless reverse, only accept first structural call? Pass attempt? Simpler: don't accept reverse unless no other? We'll not use choice 0? Use average with reversed.
      if choice==1:
         m=int(rng.integers(1,n-1)); t=float(rng.choice([0.5,0.75,1.25,1.5,2.0]))
         new_a=a.copy(); new_a[m:]*=t
         sc=fast_score(new_a)
         if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-12: best...; accepted=True
      if choice==2:
         # split both sides: prefix t, suffix 1 maybe
      if choice==3:
         width=int(max(1, n//50)); start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width)); factor=float(np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.25)))
         new_a=a.copy(); new_a[start:start+width]*=factor
         sc=fast_score(new_a)
         if sc < best_sc*(1+0.02) and (sc < best_sc or rng.random()<0.15): best...
      # also tie-break: new_a=0.5*(a+a[::-1])? evaluate; might be worse rarely accept mild
   if accepted:
       # normalize sum1 and recompute c direct if n<=3000 else fft
       best_a=normalize_sum1(best_a)
       best_c = np.convolve(best_a,best_a) if n<=3000 else fft_conv(best_a)
       best_c=np.clip(best_c,0,None); best_S=1.0; best_sc=2*n*max(best_c)
   return best_a,best_c,best_S,best_sc,accepted
```

`next_pow2(m)`:
```
def next_pow2(m):
    r=1
    while r<m: r*=2
    return r
```

`fft_convolution(a)`:
```
def fft_convolution(a):
    n=a.size
    L=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,L)[:2*n-1]
    c[ c<0 ] = 0.0
    return c
```
`fast_score(a)`:
```
def fast_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    if a.size==0: return np.inf
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12: return np.inf
    c=fft_convolution(a)
    m=float(c.max())
    return float(2*a.size*m/(s*s))
```
Note: For n large, a may have small negative from numeric? clip.

`score_from_c(n,c,S)`.

`normalize_sum1(a)`.

```
def consider_candidate(seq, best_list, max_keep=10):
   a=np.asarray(seq,float)
   if a.size==0: return

   if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): return

```

Let's implement `tilt_sequence(n, alpha, power)`:
```
def tilt_sequence(n, alpha, power):
    base=central_sequence(n)
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    a=base*np.exp(alpha*(x**power))
    s=a.sum(); if s>0: a/=s
    return a
```
Use as initial for local_search; score maybe worse but no matter.

`random_smooth_sequence(base, rng)` maybe use base central.
```
def random_smooth_sequence(n, rng, base=None):
   if base is None: base=central_sequence(n)
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   f=np.zeros(n)
   for k in range(1,5): f += rng.normal(0,1.0/k)*np.cos(2*math.pi*k*x + rng.uniform(0,2*math.pi))
   alpha=float(rng.uniform(-0.5,1.5))
   a=base*np.exp(f+alpha*x)
   a=np.clip(a,1e-12,None)
   return normalize_sum1(a)
```

`coord_scores_for_ts`:
```
def coord_scores_for_ts(c_seg, a_seg, max_unaff, S, n, old, pos, ts):
    ts = np.asarray(ts, dtype=float)
    k = ts.size
    if k == 0:
        return np.empty(0)
    dt = ts - old
    # If k==1 use scalar to avoid 2D? 2D fine.
    mat = c_seg.reshape(1, -1) + (2.0 * dt).reshape(-1, 1) * a_seg.reshape(1, -1)
    mat[:, pos] += dt * dt
    rowmax = np.max(mat, axis=1)
    if max_unaff > rowmax:
        rowmax = np.maximum(rowmax, max_unaff)
    sumnew = S - old + ts
    scores = np.full(k, np.inf, dtype=float)
    valid = (sumnew > 1e-300) & np.isfinite(rowmax)
    if np.any(valid):
        scores[valid] = (2.0 * n * rowmax[valid]) / (sumnew[valid] * sumnew[valid])
    return scores
```
If max_unaff is nan? no. This creates mat with K rows. If K=1 and n large, okay. For `max_unaff > rowmax` where rowmax array, Python scalar > array returns array; we can just `rowmax = np.maximum(rowmax, max_unaff)` always.

`coord_line_search`:
```
def coord_line_search(i, a, c, S, n, cur_score):
    old = float(a[i])
    c_seg = c[i:i+n]
    a_seg = a[i:i+n]
    # max unaff
    max_unaff = 0.0
    if i > 0:
        ml = float(np.max(c[:i]))
        if ml > max_unaff: max_unaff=ml
    end = i+n
    mlen = 2*n-1
    if end < mlen:
        mr = float(np.max(c[end:mlen]))
        if mr > max_unaff: max_unaff=mr
    mean = S / float(n)
    candidates=[old,0.0,mean]
    if old > 0.0:
       for r in (0.25,0.5,0.75,1.25,1.5,2.5,4.0): candidates.append(old*r)
    else:
       for f in (1.0,10.0,100.0,1000.0): candidates.append(mean*f)
    bound = max(1.0, 4.0*old if old>0.0 else 0.0, 10.0*mean)
    candidates.append(bound)
    # filter
    ts=[]
    for t in candidates:
       if t is None: continue
       t=float(t)
       if not np.isfinite(t) or t < 0.0: continue
       # if old small and t close old, skip duplicates
       if not ts or abs(t-ts[-1]) > 1e-14*max(1.0,abs(t)):
           ts.append(t)
    ts=np.array(ts,float)
    scores=coord_scores_for_ts(c_seg,a_seg,max_unaff,S,n,old,i,ts)
    idx=int(np.argmin(scores))
    best_t=float(ts[idx]); best_sc=float(scores[idx])
    hi=best_t
    if hi <= 1e-300:
       hi=bound if bound>1e-300 else 1.0
    else:
       hi=max(hi*2.0, bound)
    if not np.isfinite(hi) or hi < 1e-12: hi=1.0
    # expand if best near 0? no
    # golden if potential
    if best_sc < cur_score * (1.0 + 1e-7):
       lo=0.0
       phi=0.3819660112501051
       # ensure hi > lo
       if hi <= lo: hi=1.0
       # We sometimes have hi where function maybe? no.
       for _ in range(8):
           x1 = lo + phi*(hi-lo)? Standard golden with c1=hi-phi*(hi-lo), c2=lo+phi... Simpler:
           x1 = hi - (hi-lo)*0.381966
           x2 = lo + (hi-lo)*0.381966
           if x2 <= x1: x2 = x1 + 1e-12*max(1.0,hi)
           vals=coord_scores_for_ts(c_seg,a_seg,max_unaff,S,n,old,i,[x1,x2])
           f1=float(vals[0]); f2=float(vals[1])
           if f1 <= f2:
               hi=x2
               if f1 < best_sc:
                   best_sc=f1; best_t=x1
           else:
               lo=x1
               if f2 < best_sc:
                   best_sc=f2; best_t=x2
       # test endpoints maybe
       vals=coord_scores_for_ts(...[lo,hi]); update?
    if best_sc >= cur_score*(1.0-1e-12):
       return None
    if abs(best_t-old) <= 1e-13*max(1.0,abs(old)):
       return None
    return (best_t,best_sc)
```
Golden bracketing logic: Standard if f1<=f2, minimum in [lo,x2], set hi=x2; else [x1,hi], lo=x1. Here x1=lo+0.382*(hi-lo), x2=hi-0.382*(hi-lo). Good. Need update best if new. If best at endpoint 0 or hi, golden won't return exactly hi if minimum at hi? It will shrink to hi as f? If f decreases to hi, it may update best at x1/x2 close hi; candidates include bound. Good.

If best_sc is nan? filter finite? coord_scores returns inf for invalid. If all inf, argmin 0. If best_sc inf and cur finite, golden maybe; returns None. Fine.

Potential problem: When old is huge and bound smaller than old? candidates include old; hi = max(old*2, bound) maybe huge. But sum1 old≤1; okay. If during sweep S can grow and old maybe few, hi maybe few. Good.

```
def try_structural_moves(a, S, n, rng, cur_score, deadline):
    # a sum maybe 1; c not needed
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=cur_score; accepted=False
    base_seq=a

    for trial in range(24):
       if time.monotonic()>=deadline: break
       choice=int(rng.integers(0,4))
       if choice==0:
           frac=float(rng.uniform(0.1,0.9)); m=int(n*frac)
           t_prefix=float(rng.choice([0.5,0.75,1.25,1.5])); t_suffix=float(rng.choice([0.5,0.75,1.25,1.5]))
           new_a=a.copy(); new_a[:m]*=t_prefix; new_a[m:]*=t_suffix
       elif choice==1:
           frac=float(rng.uniform(0.1,0.9)); m=int(n*frac)
           t=float(rng.choice([0.6,0.8,1.25,1.5,2.0]))
           new_a=a.copy(); new_a[m:]*=t
       elif choice==2:
           width=int(max(1,n//20)); start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width))
           new_a=a.copy(); new_a[start:start+width]*=float(np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.3)))
       else:
           x? use sine tilt

       sc=fast_score(new_a)
       if np.isfinite(sc):
          if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
             best_score=sc; best_a=new_a; accepted=True
          elif sc < best_score*(1+0.02):
             # maybe accept mild with probability to escape? Could cause oscillation. Use small prob 0.05
             if rng.random() < 0.05:
                best_score=sc; best_a=new_a; accepted=True
    if not accepted:
       return a, cur_score, False
    best_a=normalize_sum1(best_a)
    # recompute cur score actual direct? Use fast_score(best_a) maybe; for coordinate direct need c, handled by local_search after return? We'll return with c=None and recompute.
    return best_a, best_score, True
```

Let's write `large_structured_search`:
```
def large_structured_search(n, rng, deadline):
    base=central_sequence(n)
    a=normalize_sum1(base)
    best=a.copy(); best_sc=fast_score(a)
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    # deterministic
    for p in (0.25,0.5,1.0,2.0):
      for alpha in (-2.0,-1.0,-0.5,0.5,1.0,2.0,3.0):
         if time>deadline: break
         new_a=base*np.exp(alpha*(x**p))
         sc=fast_score(new_a)
         if sc<best_sc: update
    for frac in (0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.8):
      for t in (0.5,0.8,1.2,1.5,2.0):
         new_a=base.copy(); new_a[int(n*frac):]*=t
         sc=fast_score; update
    rng_smooth loops:
      while time<deadline-2:
         new_a=random_smooth_sequence from base (but base central sum1)
         sc=fast_score; update
    return normalize_sum1(best), best_sc
```
Creating x**p each alpha repeated; precompute powers list? Use dict. Not necessary.

But `fast_score` for n=8192 in deterministic ~100 calls, random maybe 1000 calls. Time? 8192 FFT length16384; one forward+inverse ~ maybe 0.2ms? 1100*0.4=0.4s? Python overhead maybe 2ms? ~2s. Fine. Use large_search only 200s, may do many. Good.

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    try:
       rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
       deadline=time.monotonic()+max(1.0,float(budget_s)-10.0)
       best_list=[]
       for n in (4498,1024,2048,4096,8192):
           consider_candidate(central_sequence(n), best_list, 12)
       # height sequence
       hs = globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
       if hs is not None:
          try:
             h=np.asarray(hs,dtype=float)
             h=np.clip(h,0,1000)
             if h.size>0 and np.all(np.isfinite(h)):
                 if h.size>8192:
                    step=int(h.size//8192)+1; h=h[::step]
                 consider_candidate(h,best_list,12)
          except Exception: pass
       # large structural searches
       now=time.monotonic()
       if now < deadline - 60:
           end=min(deadline, now+90)
           a,sc=large_structured_search(4096,rng,end)
           consider_candidate(a,best_list,12)
       now=time.monotonic()
       if now < deadline - 60:
           end=min(deadline, now+90)
           a,sc=large_structured_search(8192,rng,end)
           consider_candidate(a,best_list,12)
       # local searches
       # 2048 central
       seq=central_sequence(2048)
       allocate = 500
       end=min(deadline, time.monotonic()+allocate)
       if end>time.monotonic()+20:
           a,sc=local_search_sequence(seq,rng,end)
           consider_candidate(a,best_list,12)
       # reverse 2048
       seq=central_sequence(2048)[::-1]
       end=min(deadline, time.monotonic()+300)
       if end>time.monotonic()+20:
           a,sc=local_search_sequence(seq,rng,end)
           consider_candidate(a,best_list,12)
       # 1024 central
       seq=central_sequence(1024)
       end=min(deadline, time.monotonic()+300)
       if end>...
       # tilt 2048
       seq=tilt_sequence(2048,1.0,1.0)
       end=min(deadline, time.monotonic()+150)
       # random
       seq=random_smooth_sequence(2048,rng)
       end=min(deadline, time.monotonic()+100)
       # if still time, run another random larger 4096 structural? maybe
       now=time.monotonic()
       if now < deadline-60:
           seq=central_sequence(2048)
           a,sc=local_search_sequence(seq,rng,deadline)
           consider_candidate(a,best_list,12)
       # final exact
       # sort best_list already
       final_seq=None; final_score=np.inf
       # candidates consider unique top indices
       tried=0
       for sc,a in best_list:
           if tried>=4: break
           a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
           if a.size==0: continue
           try:
               exact=evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in a])
           except Exception:
               exact=np.inf
           tried+=1
           if np.isfinite(exact) and exact<final_score:
               final_score=exact; final_seq=a
           if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2: break
       if final_seq is None:
           # use first fast candidate or central fallback
           if best_list: final_seq=np.clip(best_list[0][1],0,1000)
           else: final_seq=central_sequence(4498)
       return [float(x) for x in final_seq]
    except Exception:
       # ultimate fallback
       seq=central_sequence(4498)
       return [float(x) for x in seq]
```

Implement `try_structural_moves` top-level:
```
def try_structural_moves(a, n, rng, cur_score, deadline):
   best_a=a.copy(); best_sc=cur_score; accepted=False
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n) # could precompute? n small; okay but each call.
   for trial in range(20):
       ... new_a...
       sc=fast_score(new_a)
       if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
          best_sc=sc; best_a=new_a; accepted=True
       elif sc < best_sc*1.01 and rng.random()<0.05:
          best_sc=sc; best_a=new_a; accepted=True
   if not accepted: return a, None, False
   best_a=normalize_sum1(best_a)
   return best_a, best_sc, True
```

```python
import numpy as np
import time
import math


"""
Algorithm summary:
- Use explicit central-binomial polynomials P(z)=(1-z)^(-1/2) truncated.
  Their full convolution has early coefficients equal to 1, giving scores
  near pi/2 rather than the uniform value 2.
- Improve these and related tilts with:
    * direct coordinate-wise line search (exact quadratic updates),
    * structural split/scale perturbations,
    * large-n structured/random smooth searches using FFT convolution.
- Keep a small list of best candidates and validate the finalists with the
  official evaluate_sequence when possible.
"""


def next_pow2(m):
    r = 1
    while r < m:
        r *= 2
    return r


def normalize_sum1(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if a.size == 0:
        return a
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.zeros(a.shape, dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s


def fft_convolution(a):
    n = int(a.size)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    need = 2 * n - 1
    L = next_pow2(need)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, L)[:need]
    np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
    return c


def fast_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.inf
    c = fft_convolution(a)
    m = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 0.0:
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * a.size * m / (s * s))


def direct_convolution(a):
    # For medium n, exact direct convolution is fast and avoids FFT roundoff.
    return np.asarray(np.convolve(a, a), dtype=np.float64)


def score_from_c(n, c, s):
    if s <= 1e-300 or c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * int(n) * m / (s * s))


def central_sequence(n):
    # Coefficients of (1-z)^(-1/2): a_0=1, a_m = a_{m-1}*(2m-1)/(2m).
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[0] = 1.0
    for m in range(1, n):
        a[m] = a[m - 1] * (2.0 * m - 1.0) / (2.0 * m)
    return a


def consider_candidate(seq, best_list, max_keep=12):
    a = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 0.01:
        return
    a = a / s
    sc = fast_score(a)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        return
    item = (float(sc), a)
    best_list.append(item)
    # Manual insertion sort, no lambda.
    pos = len(best_list) - 1
    while pos > 0:
        prev = best_list[pos - 1]
        if prev[0] <= item[0]:
            break
        best_list[pos] = prev
        pos -= 1
    best_list[pos] = item
    if len(best_list) > int(max_keep):
        best_list.pop()


def tilt_sequence(n, alpha, power):
    base = central_sequence(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return base
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    a = base * np.exp(float(alpha) * np.power(x, float(power)))
    return normalize_sum1(a)


def random_smooth_sequence(n, rng, base=None):
    if base is None:
        base = central_sequence(n)
    base = np.asarray(base, dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 1:
        return normalize_sum1(base)
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    f = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for k in range(1, 5):
        f += rng.normal(0.0, 1.0 / float(k)) * np.cos(2.0 * math.pi * k * x + rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * math.pi))
    alpha = float(rng.uniform(-0.7, 1.7))
    a = base * np.exp(f + alpha * x)
    return normalize_sum1(np.clip(a, 1e-12, 1000.0))


def coord_scores_for_ts(c_seg, a_seg, max_unaff, S, n, old, pos, ts):
    ts = np.asarray(ts, dtype=np.float64)
    k = int(ts.size)
    if k == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    dt = ts - float(old)
    # For each t: new segment = c_seg + 2*(t-old)*a_seg, with extra (t-old)^2 at self.
    mat = c_seg.reshape(1, -1) + (2.0 * dt).reshape(-1, 1) * a_seg.reshape(1, -1)
    mat[:, pos] += dt * dt
    rowmax = np.max(mat, axis=1)
    rowmax = np.maximum(rowmax, float(max_unaff))
    sumnew = S - old + ts
    scores = np.full(k, np.inf, dtype=np.float64)
    valid = (sumnew > 1e-300) & np.isfinite(rowmax)
    if np.any(valid):
        scores[valid] = (2.0 * float(n) * rowmax[valid]) / (sumnew[valid] * sumnew[valid])
    return scores


def coord_line_search(i, a, c, S, n, cur_score):
    i = int(i)
    old = float(a[i])
    c_seg = c[i:i + n]
    a_seg = a[i:i + n]
    total_len = 2 * n - 1

    max_unaff = 0.0
    if i > 0:
        ml = float(np.max(c[:i]))
        if ml > max_unaff:
            max_unaff = ml
    end = i + n
    if end < total_len:
        mr = float(np.max(c[end:total_len]))
        if mr > max_unaff:
            max_unaff = mr

    mean = S / float(n)
    candidates = [old, 0.0, mean]
    if old > 0.0:
        for r in (0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.25, 1.5, 2.5, 4.0):
            candidates.append(old * r)
    else:
        for f in (1.0, 10.0, 100.0, 1000.0):
            candidates.append(mean * f)

    bound = max(1.0, 4.0 * old if old > 0.0 else 0.0, 10.0 * mean)
    candidates.append(bound)

    ts = []
    for t in candidates:
        try:
            t = float(t)
        except Exception:
            continue
        if not np.isfinite(t) or t < 0.0:
            continue
        if not ts or abs(t - ts[-1]) > 1e-14 * max(1.0, abs(t)):
            ts.append(t)
    ts = np.asarray(ts, dtype=np.float64)
    if ts.size == 0:
        return None

    scores = coord_scores_for_ts(c_seg, a_seg, max_unaff, S, n, old, i, ts)
    if scores.size == 0:
        return None
    idx = int(np.argmin(scores))
    best_t = float(ts[idx])
    best_sc = float(scores[idx])

    # Only do a finer golden search if there is a plausible chance of improving.
    if np.isfinite(best_sc) and best_sc < cur_score * (1.0 + 1e-7):
        hi = max(best_t * 2.0, bound)
        if hi <= 1e-12:
            hi = 1.0
        lo = 0.0
        phi = 0.3819660112501051
        for _ in range(8):
            if hi - lo <= 1e-15 * max(1.0, hi):
                break
            x1 = hi - (hi - lo) * phi
            x2 = lo + (hi - lo) * phi
            if x2 <= x1:
                x2 = x1 + 1e-12 * max(1.0, hi)
            vals = coord_scores_for_ts(c_seg, a_seg, max_unaff, S, n, old, i, [x1, x2])
            f1 = float(vals[0]) if vals.size > 0 else np.inf
            f2 = float(vals[1]) if vals.size > 1 else np.inf
            if f1 <= f2:
                hi = x2
                if f1 < best_sc:
                    best_sc = f1
                    best_t = x1
            else:
                lo = x1
                if f2 < best_sc:
                    best_sc = f2
                    best_t = x2

        # Endpoints can be the practical minimum.
        if lo > 0.0 or hi < bound:
            vals = coord_scores_for_ts(c_seg, a_seg, max_unaff, S, n, old, i, [lo, hi])
            if vals.size > 0:
                f1 = float(vals[0])
                if f1 < best_sc:
                    best_sc = f1
                    best_t = lo
            if vals.size > 1:
                f2 = float(vals[1])
                if f2 < best_sc:
                    best_sc = f2
                    best_t = hi

    if not np.isfinite(best_sc):
        return None
    if best_sc >= cur_score * (1.0 - 1e-12):
        return None
    if abs(best_t - old) <= 1e-13 * max(1.0, abs(old)):
        return None
    return (best_t, best_sc)


def try_structural_moves(a, n, rng, cur_score, deadline):
    best_a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    best_score = float(cur_score)
    accepted = False
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return best_a, best_score, False

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    for _ in range(24):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break
        choice = int(rng.integers(0, 4))
        new_a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()

        if choice == 0:
            m = int(rng.integers(1, n))
            tp = float(rng.choice([0.5, 0.75, 1.25, 1.5]))
            ts = float(rng.choice([0.5, 0.75, 1.25, 1.5]))
            new_a[:m] *= tp
            new_a[m:] *= ts
        elif choice == 1:
            m = int(rng.integers(1, n))
            t = float(rng.choice([0.6, 0.8, 1.25, 1.5, 2.0]))
            new_a[m:] *= t
        elif choice == 2:
            width = int(max(1, n // 20))
            start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width)))
            stop = min(n, start + width)
            new_a[start:stop] *= float(exp if False else np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.30)))
        else:
            power = float(rng.choice([0.35, 0.5, 1.0, 1.8]))
            alpha = float(rng.uniform(-1.2, 1.8))
            new_a *= np.exp(alpha * np.power(x, power))

        sc = fast_score(new_a)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_score = sc
                best_a = new_a
                accepted = True
            elif sc < best_score * 1.01:
                # Occasional non-monotone jump to escape very flat regions.
                if rng.random() < 0.04:
                    best_score = sc
                    best_a = new_a
                    accepted = True

    if not accepted:
        return np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy(), float(cur_score), False
    best_a = normalize_sum1(best_a)
    return best_a, float(best_score), True


def local_search_sequence(a0, rng, deadline, verbose=False):
    a = normalize_sum1(a0)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n <= 0:
        return a, np.inf
    S = 1.0
    if n <= 3000:
        c = direct_convolution(a)
    else:
        c = fft_convolution(a)
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
    cur_score = score_from_c(n, c, S)
    if not np.isfinite(cur_score):
        return a, np.inf

    best = a.copy()
    best_score = cur_score
    no_improve = 0
    sweeps = 0
    max_sweeps = 1200

    while time.monotonic() < deadline and sweeps < max_sweeps:
        order = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
        if sweeps % 2 == 0:
            start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n)))
            order = np.roll(order, start)
        else:
            order = rng.permutation(n)

        improved = 0
        for idx in order:
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                break
            res = coord_line_search(int(idx), a, c, S, n, cur_score)
            if res is None:
                continue
            t, new_score = res
            i = int(idx)
            old = float(a[i])
            dt = float(t) - old
            if dt == 0.0:
                continue
            # Apply exact update to the convolution.
            c[i:i + n] += (2.0 * dt) * a[i:i + n]
           /self_add = dt * dt
            if 0 <= 2 * i < c.size:
                c[2 * i] += self_add
            a[i] = float(t)
            S += dt
            cur_score = float(new_score)
            improved += 1
            if cur_score < best_score - 1e-13:
                best_score = cur_score
                best = a.copy()

        sweeps += 1

        # Rescale to sum 1. The objective is scale invariant.
        if S <= 1e-300:
            break
        factor = 1.0 / S
        a *= factor
        c *= (factor * factor)
        S = 1.0
        np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
        cur_score = score_from_c(n, c, S)
        if np.isfinite(cur_score) and cur_score < best_score - 1e-13:
            best_score = cur_score
            best = a.copy()

        if improved == 0:
            no_improve += 1
        else:
            no_improve = 0

        if no_improve >= 2:
            new_a, proposed_score, accepted = try_structural_moves(a, n, rng, cur_score, deadline)
            if accepted:
                a = normalize_sum1(new_a)
                S = 1.0
                if n <= 3000:
                    c = direct_convolution(a)
                else:
                    c = fft_convolution(a)
                np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
                cur_score = score_from_c(n, c, S)
                no_improve = 0
                if np.isfinite(cur_score) and cur_score < best_score - 1e-13:
                    best_score = cur_score
                    best = a.copy()
            else:
                if no_improve >= 8:
                    break
        elif no_improve == 0 and sweeps % 50 == 0 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 5.0:
            # Periodic exact recompute to remove drift.
            if n <= 3000:
                c = direct_convolution(a)
            else:
                c = fft_convolution(a)
            np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
            cur_score = score_from_c(n, c, 1.0)
            if not np.isfinite(cur_score):
                break

    bs = float(np.sum(best))
    if bs > 1e-300:
        best = best / bs
    return best, float(best_score)


def large_structured_search(n, rng, deadline):
    n = int(n)
    base = central_sequence(n)
    a0 = normalize_sum1(base)
    best = a0.copy()
    best_score = fast_score(a0)
    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
        return a0, np.inf

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)

    # Deterministic tilts and splits.
    powers = (0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 1.8)
    alphas = (-2.0, -1.0, -0.5, -0.2, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 1.8, 3.0)
    for p in powers:
        rp = np.power(x, float(p))
        for alpha in alphas:
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                break
            cand = base * np.exp(float(alpha) * rp)
            sc = fast_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best = cand

    fracs = (0.15, 0.25, 0.35, 0.50, 0.65, 0.80)
    scales = (0.5, 0.75, 1.25, 1.6, 2.2)
    for fr in fracs:
        m = int(max(1, min(n - 1, n * fr)))
        for t in scales:
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                break
            cand = base.copy()
            cand[m:] *= float(t)
            sc = fast_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best = cand

    # Power-law bases.
    idx = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    for p in (0.25, 0.35, 0.5, 0.65, 0.85, 1.1):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break
        cand = np.power(idx, -float(p))
        sc = fast_score(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best = cand

    # Random smooth variations.
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 1.0:
        cand = random_smooth_sequence(n, rng, base=base)
        sc = fast_score(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best = cand

    return normalize_sum1(best), float(best_score)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    try:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
        deadline = time.monotonic() + max(1.0, float(budget_s) - 10.0)
        best_list = []

        # Strong explicit fallbacks / starting points.
        fallback_ns = (4498, 1024, 2048, 4096, 8192)
        for n in fallback_ns:
            consider_candidate(central_sequence(n), best_list, 14)

        # Use any provided previous construction if useful.
        hs = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
        if hs is not None:
            try:
                h = np.asarray(hs, dtype=np.float64)
                if h.size > 0 and np.all(np.isfinite(h)):
                    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1000.0)
                    if h.size > 8192:
                        step = int(h.size // 8192) + 1
                        h = h[::step]
                    consider_candidate(h, best_list, 14)
            except Exception:
                pass

        if best_list:
            print("initial best fast score = %.9f (n=%d)" % (best_list[0][0], best_list[0][1].size))
        else:
            print("initial candidates failed; using pure central fallback")

        # Large-n structured/random search (no coordinate sweeps, FFT-based).
        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 60.0:
            end = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 80.0)
            a, _ = large_structured_search(4096, rng, end)
            consider_candidate(a, best_list, 14)
        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 60.0:
            end = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 80.0)
            a, _ = large_structured_search(8192, rng, end)
            consider_candidate(a, best_list, 14)

        # Coordinate/local search on medium lengths.
        # Primary basin: central n=2048.
        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 30.0:
            end = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 560.0)
            a, _ = local_search_sequence(central_sequence(2048), rng, end)
            consider_candidate(a, best_list, 14)

        # Mirror basin: reversed central n=2048.
        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 30.0:
            end = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 320.0)
            a, _ = local_search_sequence(central_sequence(2048)[::-1], rng, end)
            consider_candidate(a, best_list, 14)

        # Smaller n can have smaller finite-N corrections and many sweeps fit.
        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 30.0:
            end = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 280.0)
            a, _ = local_search_sequence(central_sequence(1024), rng, end)
            consider_candidate(a, best_list, 14)

        # Tilted basin to escape the central-binomial local structure.
        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 30.0:
            end = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 170.0)
            a, _ = local_search_sequence(tilt_sequence(2048, 1.0, 1.0), rng, end)
            consider_candidate(a, best_list, 14)

        # Random smooth basin.
        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 30.0:
            end = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 110.0)
            a, _ = local_search_sequence(random_smooth_sequence(2048, rng), rng, end)
            consider_candidate(a, best_list, 14)

        # If time remains, continue on the best medium basin.
        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 30.0:
            end = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 180.0)
            a, _ = local_search_sequence(central_sequence(2048), rng, end)
            consider_candidate(a, best_list, 14)

        # Final validation with the official evaluator when available.
        final_seq = None
        final_exact = np.inf
        tried = 0
        for sc, a in best_list:
            if tried >= 4:
                break
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2.0:
                break
            a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if a.size == 0:
                continue
            if a.size > 9000:
                step = int(a.size // 8000) + 1
                a = a[::step]
            try:
                exact = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in a])
            except Exception:
                exact = np.inf
            tried += 1
            if np.isfinite(exact) and exact < final_exact:
                final_exact = float(exact)
                final_seq = a

        if final_seq is None:
            if best_list:
                final_seq = np.clip(best_list[0][1], 0.0, 1000.0)
                if final_seq.size > 9000:
                    step = int(final_seq.size // 8000) + 1
                    final_seq = final_seq[::step]
            else:
                final_seq = central_sequence(4498)

        final_seq = np.asarray(final_seq, dtype=np.float64)
        final_seq = np.clip(final_seq, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(np.sum(final_seq))
        if s > 1e-300:
            final_seq = final_seq / s
        print("final sequence length = %d" % final_seq.size)
        print("final exact/log score = %.9f" % (final_exact if np.isfinite(final_exact) else fast_score(final_seq)))
        return [float(x) for x in final_seq]

    except Exception:
        # Guaranteed safe fallback.
        seq = np.asarray(central_sequence(4498), dtype=np.float64)
        seq = np.clip(seq, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(np.sum(seq))
        if s > 0.0:
            seq = seq / s
        return [float(x) for x in seq]
```
